美國股市：市場樂觀預期美伊協議達成，美股再創新高
五月美股聚焦於 AI 產業進展與供應鏈訊息，且投資人樂觀預期美伊有望達成協議，並使荷姆茲
海峽恢復正常通行， 推動標普與納指 再創歷史新高 。指數上揚主要由 蘋果、美光、超微半導體 、
Alphabet、特斯拉、禮來、輝達、英特爾、思科等科技股帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括 Meta、微
軟、沃爾瑪、萬事達卡、直覺、網飛、埃克森美孚、碩騰、新紀元能源、康菲石油等，市場焦點回歸
AI 贏家與受惠的供應鏈。
美國 5 月製造業 PMI 自上月的 54.5 上升至 55.3，服務業 PMI 自上月的 51.0 下降至 50.9，低於

市場預期的 51.2。企業活動維持溫和擴張，但產業表現分化，其中製造業受到企業補庫存帶動，而
服務業略微放緩，高物價與不確定性持續壓抑消費與企業支出意願。此外，就業市場同樣出現分化，
製造業就業小幅增加，但整體民間企業聘僱增速下降，企業招聘態度趨於保守。價格方面，供應鏈延
誤與能源價格上漲推升企業成本。綜合來看，雖然美國經濟的確具有韌性，但通膨壓力仍持續累積。
美國 5 月密大消費者信心指數顯示，由於高油價與通膨疑慮升溫，消費者信心再創新低。密大消
費者信心指數終值由初值的 48.2 下修至 44.8，現況指標由初值的 47.8 下修至 45.8，預期指標則由
48.5 下滑至 44.1。消費者 1 年期通膨預期上升至 4.8%，且 5-10 年中長期通膨預期則大幅升至 3.9%，
全面高於預期。油價與生活成本壓力持續惡化下，消費者不僅對當前經濟與財務狀況評價惡化，對未
來景氣與通膨前景亦轉趨悲觀。近期 FOMC 會議紀錄顯示部分官員已開始討論移除政策聲明中的寬
鬆傾向指引，反映聯準會內部同樣對通膨風險的警戒升溫，政策立場轉趨偏鷹。

AI 投資熱潮推動標普 500 企業現金配置從庫藏股轉向資本支出 。 市場共識預估AI 雲端業者(亞馬
遜、Alphabet、META、微軟、甲骨文)2026 年資本支出可望超過 7,000 億美元，為強化現金流量，
這些公司只好削減庫藏股。近期財報季已突顯由庫藏股轉向資本支出與研發的趨勢，大型科技巨擘除
非選擇大規模舉債，否則未來一年企業庫藏股動能恐難明顯回升，讓未來一年的股市波動性擴大。
目前標普 500 本益比已升至約 26 倍，前瞻本益比也在 22 倍位置，評價面已偏高，加上新任聯
準會主席就任後政策不確定性、美伊談判反覆、AI 主題擁擠度偏高等多重變數，市場拉回風險仍需
留意。然而基本面仍具支撐，故雖然短期需考量估值、通膨與地緣政治風險，但中長期則維持對美股
及 AI 產業正面看法，操作上建議以分批、長期角度布局。

日本股市：AI 題材提振日股表現；日本央行 6 月升息機率上升
五月日股受惠於 AI 產業表現強勁，日經 225 指數站上 6 萬 5 千點歷史新高。市場主要由軟銀、
村田製作所、瑞可利控股、瑞薩電子、鎧俠、東京威力科創、三菱 UFJ 金融、索尼、住友商事等公
司帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括三菱重工、愛德萬測試、藤倉、三井物產、豪雅、丸紅、任天堂、
豐田、Disco、富士通等。市場焦點回歸 AI 主題，以及升息可能帶動的金融股。
日本 4 月出口年增 14.8%。弱勢日圓對出口帶來挹注，而實質出口年增 3.4%、連續兩個月維持

正成長，顯示出口量的成長亦是推動出口上行因素之一 。引導出口成長的重要品項包含半導體電子零
組件及非鐵金屬，其中半導體等電子零組件連續八個月的雙位數成長，就是受惠於 AI 產業需求。
日本 4 月 CPI 年增幅由前月的 1.5%下降至 1.4%，扣除新鮮食品的 CPI 年增率則由 1.8%上升至
1.4%，本月通膨的走弱為政策性補貼所影響。其中商品價格年增率上升至 1.7%，服務價格年增率則
降至 0.9%。展望後市，日本政府很可能繼續以財政手段平滑 CPI 波動。首相高市早苗於 5/25 宣布，
為因應長期化的能源成本飆漲 ， 日本政府將編列規模約為3 兆日圓的追加預算案，實際內容包括汽油
補貼、電力及瓦斯費補貼。高市同時承諾不會增加整體政府借貸，試圖緩解財政壓力憂慮。
貨幣政策方面，市場預期日銀將在 6 月啟動升息。近期日銀委員小枝淳子談話，提及應以適當地
速度調升政策利率以因應物價上漲，認為中東局勢惡化導致物價上漲的風險正在增加，然而小枝淳子
於上月會議認為應維持政策利率不變。先前美國財長貝森特表示，日本經濟基本面強勁，日圓過度波

動並不可取，相信日本央行將會採取必要行動。市場預期日本央行於 6 月啟動升息(下次會議時間為
6/10~6/11)，全年至少有 2 碼升息空間。近期可觀察日本十年期公債殖利率飆升來到 2.7%，對照美
國十年期公債殖利率仍在 4.5%，雙方利差持續縮減，有利日圓守穩 160 關卡。
日本 4 月外國人入境人數年減 5.5%至 369.2 萬人次，中國訪日旅客年減 56.8%至 33.1 萬人，連
續五個月出現雙位數的衰退，反映自去年日中關係惡化，中國遊客數維持於 30 萬人左右區間，但整
體外籍觀光客仍在單月 350~380 萬人次的高檔位置，顯示對日本觀光產業造成的負面影響有限。
日本經濟受惠於 AI 產業擴張，加上日本政府對 17 項戰略產業政策支撐明確，然而股市短期面對
中東衝突不確定性及漲多拉回風險，建議投資人可留意中東情勢 、 補充預算審議進度及日銀6 月會議
釋放的政策訊號，把握利空拉回機會、以長線角度、分批佈局日股。

新興亞洲股市：半導體與油價角力，亞洲市場分化表現
亞洲股市 5 月因中東情勢的和緩在科技類股的帶領下反彈，截至 5/26，亞洲不含日本指數
(MXAPJ) 過去一個月上漲 5.59%。南韓股市漲幅居前，KOSPI 指數單月上漲 21.19%。台灣加權指
數表現居次，上漲 12.10%。中國股市表現平平，上證指數上漲 1.78%，國企指數不漲反跌，下跌
2.56%。MSCI 東協指數受惠於美伊情勢和緩，5 月上漲 2.15%，但各國表現不盡相同。原本受惠於
油價上漲的馬來西亞及印尼由漲轉跌，其中印尼股市遭到外資短時間內在股市大舉提款，股匯雙殺，

單月下跌 12.95%，亞股中最差。新加坡、泰國、菲律賓則有 3-6%的漲幅。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌
0.55%，印度作為能源淨進口國，5 月上旬高企的國際油價加劇了市場對其國內通膨的擔憂，使得印
度央行在貨幣政策操作上的降息空間受到壓制，也壓抑了股市。
5 月亞洲製造業呈現以科技出口為核心的兩極分化格局。台灣在 AI 算力與全球半導體供應鏈強
勁拉動下，PMI 飆升至 60.3 領跑全亞洲；南韓則在外需復甦與記憶體晶片需求帶動下展開 V 型反
彈，一路回升至 53.6。印度雖自高檔拉回至 54.3，經濟在高油價的干擾下雖能維持擴張，但卻連續
放緩 。東協因歐美消費降溫 、 通膨壓制 ， 動能連月放緩至 50.7 的榮枯線邊緣； 中國大陸則持續在 50.3
微幅擴張處橫盤拉鋸，反映整體內需復甦依舊沉悶。
亞洲經濟呈現典型的 「科技強、傳統弱」格局，台、韓、兩國受惠於結構性轉型紅利而氣勢如虹，
雖也共同面臨中東衝突引發的原物料與運費飆升等成本通膨隱憂，但影響有限；相反地，缺乏科技大
潮加持的東協、印度與中國，在全球高利率與需求放緩的環境下，正提早面臨景氣停滯的嚴峻挑戰。

台、韓兩國經濟擴張具韌性，顯示全球算力基礎建設正處於實質出貨期。由於地緣政治與中東情
勢陰霾未散，資金將更傾向流入具有「實質獲利高成長」與「定價能力」的東北亞半導體巨頭。台股
加權指數與南韓 KOSPI 的強勢格局具備基本面支撐，高昂的運費與原物料成本雖是隱憂，但先進製
程與高階記憶體具備將成本轉嫁給歐美科技巨擘的能力。印度 PMI 持續放緩，股市將受高油價壓制，
印度的核心邏輯在於 「長線結構性轉型」 。未來只要中東地緣情勢未進一步惡化，國際油價能逐步回
穩，印度央行的貨幣政策擔憂將減輕。預期印度股市在震盪調整之後，長線仍有投資機會。中國製造
業 PMI 與股市的橫盤疲軟顯示內需消費信心的提振需要更多時間。未來可能的發展仍維持「以時間
換取空間」的築底格局，指數上漲空間有限。

科技類股：AI 資本支出全線擴散，企業獲利上行週期確立多頭韌性
全球科技板塊在 AI 基礎設施資本支出持續投入的背景下，正經歷結構性的架構轉型與產業鏈整
體獲利及估值上調。從大型雲端 CSP 業者的長期資本支出規劃，到硬體、網通與半導體設備大廠的
板塊效應外溢，均顯示 AI 需求具有實質支撐。儘管總體經濟環境面臨美債殖利率上行與高利率環境
的挑戰 ， 但科技類股目前正處於明確的獲利上行週期 ，企業獲利的增長預期將有效緩解估值修正壓力 。
根據彭博智庫與 S&P Global 的最新預測，全球大型雲端業者在 2026 年的總資本支出預計將達

到 8,200 億美元，較先前預估的 7,500 億美元出現顯著上修。在 2026 至 2030 年的長線格局下，全
球六大領先 CSP 業者的累計 AI 資本支出將接近 5 兆美元。其中，亞馬遜在 2026 年的資本支出預計
以 2,000 億美元位居首位，微軟與 Alphabet 緊隨其後。雖然高額支出在短期內可能對自由現金流造
成暫時性壓抑，但隨著基礎建設規模擴大，預期將帶動獲利自2028 年起加速成長；亞馬遜亦設定於
2029 年達成 1 兆美元的營收目標。
而 5 月以來的財報數據也都顯示 ，AI 資本支出除了算力晶片之外，也進一步擴散至連接端與製造
設備端。其中輝達單季營收將挑戰 780 億至 800 億美元。毛利預計將穩定維持在 75%左右的高檔。
思科單季營收年增 12%至 158.4 億美元 ， 淨利年增35%至 33.7 億美元 。 受惠於大型雲端業者對基礎
建設的投入，思科將全年 AI 相關訂單預估由原本的 50 億美元大幅上調至 90 億美元。應用材料第 2
季營收達 79.1 億美元，獲利超市場預期，且第3 季財測達 89.5 億美元。執行長預期 2026 年設備業

務將成長三成以上，這些公司的財報都顯示 AI 投資由算力端向連接端及設備端外溢的趨勢明確，良
好的基本面已經成為科技類股的成長基石。
市場正擔憂債券殖利率走高與高利率環境對科技股估值造成的壓力，但我們認為，應從估值面與
企業獲利週期進行評估。當前的總體經濟與市場結構與 2021 年的緊縮週期有些類似，但存在本質上
的差異。在 2021 年前後，聯準會為快速飆升的通膨而啟動升息，科技股因而下跌，但當時那斯達克
100 指數的預估本益比高達 35 倍，且企業獲利正處於下行週期。而目前那斯達克 100 指數預估本益
比約僅為 25.6 倍低於當時，且企業獲利更明確處於上升週期。短線上，殖利率維持高檔確實會讓科
技股產生技術性的獲利了結與波動，但在長期 AI 資本支出結構性成長與整體企業獲利步入上升週期
的雙重支撐下 ， 高利率環境對科技股長線評價的威脅將逐漸緩解。 建議投資人關注整體產業獲利全面
提升的帶來的結構性機會。

台灣股市：先進製程帶動結構性擴張，類股良性輪動構築中長線多頭
台灣 4 月外銷訂單金額達 874.5 億美元，創歷年同月新高，年增率高達 48.1%。受到 AI、高效
能運算及雲端資料服務需求的強勁拉動 ， 其中電子產品與資訊通信產品訂單表現尤為顯著 ， 預期未來
數月外銷訂單可望穩定維持在每月 800 億美元的水準。在供應鏈「去中化」趨勢下，全球高端製造
需求正高度向台灣集中 。 半導體持續擴產亦帶動本土精密機械與檢測設備訂單增長 ， 動能已逐步外溢
至傳統產業，使整體經濟走向更全面的復甦。
在外銷訂單高度成長的背後，正是因為台積電在先進製程與封裝技術上確立了絕對領先地位帶動

了整個台灣上下游產業鏈。台積電技術論壇揭示，在 AI 驅動下，2030 年全球半導體市場規模將達
1.5 兆美元，其中 AI 將貢獻 55%以上的營收。目前全球雲端服務供應商（CSP）對輝達 Blackwell
架構的強烈需求，高度仰賴台積電先進封裝產能的釋放。台積電的 2 奈米（N2）製成即將量產，更
先進的製程預計於 2026 年下半年投產，並著手規劃埃米世代製程。反觀英特爾與三星目前仍在先進
製程節點與良率之間掙扎，台積電不僅在技術進程上領先一至兩個世代，更透過良率高達 98%的
CoWoS 先進封裝建立技術壁壘，短期內對手在技術成熟度、量產規模與客戶信任度上皆難以競爭。
基本面的強勁表現反映在台股的籌碼與盤勢結構上 。 隨著5 月 21 日輝達財報化解市場觀望心理 ，
三大法人全面翻多，單日合計買超達 850.51 億元，其中外資單日買超即佔了 603.41 億元，成交值
顯著放大 。 此波修正成功洗淨市場的短線浮額 。 資金從漲多的記憶體板塊撤出 ， 流入基期較低的面板、
載板、 被動元件等產業， 盤面呈現健康且偏多的類股輪動特徵 。 資金不再僅僅獨攬於少數AI 權值股，

而是向各受惠供應鏈上下游擴散，構築了多頭的廣度。
在地緣政治上，5 月川習會整體會談並無重大協議，中美脫鉤的大趨勢持續。中美雙方在台灣問
題上維持各自表述，中方警告「台獨」與台海和平水火不容；美方則重申對台政策維持不變，雙方沒
有重大改變。在全球 CSP 廠商對算力需求尚未觸頂的基本面下，台灣高端製造與半導體供應鏈的核
心地位 ， 並未受到政治會談結果的實質干擾 。 輝達財報行情與訂單表現依然反映出算力稀缺的市場共
識，地緣政治風險僅止於情緒面擾動，未動搖產業趨勢。 台灣在全球 AI 與高效能運算資本支出浪潮
中，憑藉半導體先進製程與封裝技術，展現出極強的定價權與不可替代性。這使得總體經濟指標表現
亮眼，且擴張動能正由科技業外溢至傳統產業。台股呈現健康的類股輪動格局，地緣政治政治雜音並
未動搖高端製造向台灣集中的趨勢，中長線維持看多格局。

拉美市場：巴西總統大選前料將震盪，後市中性看待
5 月以來拉美股市表現相對疲弱，主因隨著 10 月巴西總統大選逐步逼近，選情不確定性升溫，
導致最大經濟體巴西股市出現震盪，同時美伊和談狀況膠著，油價持續維持高檔，帶動整體拉美國家
通膨預期升溫，降息預期縮窄，股市上漲動能便受到壓抑。
回顧歷史，巴西股市於大選前半年走勢普遍不佳，過去五次選舉前六個月MSCI 巴西指數報酬中
位數為負 4%，其中 2014 年與 2022 年兩次大選年更以全年下跌作收，顯示選情雜音升高之際，投
資人多於投票前轉趨觀望。在大選過後，巴西股市報酬率則取決於是否由資本市場偏好的候選人當

選，若是，則股市大多上漲，若否，則股市大多下跌，巴西股市 5 月來的跌幅就與此相關。
本次巴西總統大選中，相對受到資本市場偏好的候選人波索納洛，近期捲入醜聞，導致其支持度
大幅滑落，其被指控與涉案巴西史上最大銀行詐欺案 Banco Master 的前執行長關係密切，並涉及收
受其資金用於拍攝家族紀錄片。受此影響，民調機構 AtlasIntel 5 月 19 日最新民調顯示，在第二輪
對決調查中，魯拉以 48.9%支持度領先波索納洛的 41.8%，波索納洛由原先小幅領先轉為落後約 7
個百分點。由於市場對偏左派的魯拉財政與政策路線疑慮較深，民調再度倒向魯拉，連帶導致巴西股
市表現承壓。
另一方面，受美伊戰爭影響，西德州原油價格持續維持高檔，帶動巴西輸入性通膨壓力上升，市
場對通膨回落的信心隨之轉弱。根據巴西央行截至 5 月 22 日的 Focus 市場調查，市場預期 2026 年
消費者物價指數預期中位數已由四週前的 4.86% 升至 5.04%，進一步突破 4.50%的通膨目標上緣；
受此影響，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 基準利率的預期亦由四週前的 13.00%上修至 13.25%，意味市場

預期下半年降息幅度僅約 1 個百分點，降息預期幅度持續收窄，連帶限制股市評價向上修正的空間。
然而儘管受到選舉及通膨升溫兩股承壓力量，高盛最新報告仍對於巴西股市中長線維持加碼評
等，看多理由在於，巴西股市對本地利率的負相關程度為新興市場中之最，在過去降息循環中，股市
於首次降息後往往出現強勁漲勢，而目前巴西市場仍處於降息循環中；此外，今年以來外資對巴西股
市淨流入居新興市場之冠，亦有利股市支撐。
展望後市，降息循環有利巴西股市支撐，然而考量巴西股市於大選前表現普遍不佳，同時油價高
檔推升通膨預期、降息幅度持續收窄的情況下，指數上檔空間恐受壓抑，故我們對於拉美市場後市目
前抱持中性看法。

能源類股：雖仍供不應求，惟油價料將隨談判進度震盪走低
當前油價與能源類股表現仍取決於美伊和談進展， 5 月 23 日川普宣稱一份結束戰事並重啟荷
莫茲海峽的備忘錄已大致談定，將採先確立大方向原則，後續再就細節分軌協商的模式來結束戰爭，
伊朗官媒指出，荷莫茲海峽通行與核子相關議題，將分別在備忘錄簽署後 30 天及 60 天進行談判，
談判期間美國將分階段豁免伊朗制裁。然而截至最新消息，美伊雙方在荷莫茲海峽主權與通行收費、
鈾濃縮是否完全停止等核心議題上仍存在明顯分歧，故該份備忘錄能否順利簽署仍具不確定性。
受到荷莫茲海峽持續封鎖影響，IEA（國際能源署）5 月月報指出，4 月全球石油供給量再度下

降每日 180 萬桶至每日 9,510 萬桶，下降主因波斯灣國家停產量持續上升，不過美國、巴西、加
拿大等國家持續增加出口，也為全球油市提供部分緩解，IEA 將 2026 年美洲供給成長上修逾每日
60 萬桶至平均每日 150 萬桶，大西洋盆地原油出口也自 2 月以來增加每日 350 萬桶。
此外全球石油需求量下滑，也部分抵銷供給缺口。IEA 將 2026 年全球石油需求下修至年減每
日 42 萬桶，較開戰前的預測減少了每日 130 萬桶，產業面看以石化與航空產業需求下滑幅度最
深，國家別中，中國 4 月海運原油進口量相較 2 月大幅減少每日 360 萬桶，日韓印亦分別縮減每日
190 萬、100 萬與 76 萬桶。全球石油需求下降，主因中東石油供給受阻，導致部分石化公司被迫
減產，此外部分也跟中國經濟表現疲弱，導致的需求下滑有關。
然而儘管全球石油需求量下滑 ， 非中東地區國家也持續增產 ， 全球石油市場仍面臨每日約750 萬
桶的供給缺口，受此影響， IEA 統計全球可見庫存 3 月減少 1.29 億桶，4 月再減 1.17 億桶，

高盛則預估 5 月以來全球可見石油庫存以每日 870 萬桶的創紀錄速度被消耗。
但值得注意的是，儘管全球石油市場面臨嚴重供不應求，理應有利油價上漲，然而整個 5 月西
德州原油價格卻仍維持在每桶 80 至 110 美元區間震盪，並未進一步上漲。高盛指出，油價上漲受
阻，主因經濟合作暨發展組織(OECD)國家以每日 130 萬桶速度釋出戰略石油儲備，填補了部分缺
口，同時中國原油進口量自 2 月 27 日以來大減每日 430 萬桶，使市場上對於可售原油的爭奪趨
緩，連帶使油價漲幅受到受抑。
展望後市油價，EIA（美國能源資訊署）預估布蘭特原油價格 5、6 月均價將約為 106 美元，
隨後續海峽通行恢復，第四季油價可望回落至每桶 89 美元，惟若海峽重開延後至六月底，則後續
油價將可能較此預測在上升每桶 20 美元。整體而言，後市油價及能源類股表現，仍將視美伊談判
進度而定，然而考量美伊戰事持續朝收斂方向前進，預料後續油價將隨談判進度震盪走低，相較不利
能源類股表現，在缺乏進一步利多支撐的狀況下，我們對於能源類股後市目前保守看待。

黃金類股：黃金表現仍承壓，惟全球央行買盤漸強
5 月受到美伊戰爭帶動美債殖利率上行影響，黃金表現仍承壓，然而我們觀察到來自全球央行的
買盤逐漸轉為強勁，顯示各國央行對於逢低增持黃金的興趣仍存，有利為金價帶來支撐，預料來自央
行的買盤將可使金價保持在區間內震盪整理，等待美伊衝突緩解，殖利率上行的壓力消退。
央行買盤方面，高盛於5 月報告大幅上修其央行購金量預估，主因其推估模型自 2025 年 8 月起
系統性低估各國央行購金量，經參數修正後，高盛預估各國央行購金量 12 個月移動平均值由原本估
計的每月 29 公噸調升至每月 50 公噸，單月修正幅度更大，1 月由 12 公噸上修至 66 公噸、2 月由 2

公噸上修至 41 公噸，2026 全年央行購金量則預估為平均每月 60 公噸。
世界黃金協會數據亦呼應此預估，根據世界黃金協會統計 ，2026 年第 1 季全球央行淨購金達 244
公噸，季增 17%，超越前季與五年均值。在已申報買方中，波蘭央行 Q1 增持 31 公噸為最大買家，
烏茲別克央行則增持 25 公噸、哈薩克央行 12 公噸緊隨其後。此外中國人行持續增持黃金動作也未
停歇，在 4 月中國人行單月增持 8 公噸黃金，創下 2024 年 12 月以來單月新高，並且是連續第 18
個月增持黃金儲備，帶動其官方持有量上升至 2,322 公噸，占外匯儲備 9%。從以上數據可以看出，
各國央行在近幾個月金價走疲的階段，仍持續加大力度增持黃金儲備。
在黃金 ETF 資金面亦可見到轉折出現的跡象，繼 3 月黃金 ETF 出現顯著資金流出後，4 月全球
實物黃金 ETF 轉為出現 66 億美元的資金流入，所有地區在均呈正向流入，推動全球黃金 ETF 黃金持
有量從 3 月底約 4,092 公噸反彈 45 公噸至 4,137 公噸，達到歷史第三高 ， 僅略低於2/27 創下的 4,176

公噸紀錄，其中歐洲基金表現最強，4 月流入 37 億美元使年初至今累計由負轉正，主要受地緣政治
風險升溫、英國央行立場不如預期鷹派及本地股市回跌驅動。亞洲則延續第 8 個月淨流入趨勢，4 月
增加 18 億美元，其中香港受惠於新 ETF 產品掛牌上市錄得 7.32 億美元單月歷史新高，中國在地緣
緊張 、 殖利率下行及官方持續宣布增持黃金的背景下亦流入 4.98 億美元。由黃金 ETF 數據亦可看出，
市場資金對於逢低增持黃金配置的興趣正在上升。
展望後市，在國際地緣政治紛擾不斷的狀況下 ，仍可預期央行買盤與個人投資組合中分散風險的
需求可望延續，有利為金價帶來支撐，然而在高油價將可能使利率維持在高檔較久的環境下，黃金
ETF 資金流入恐難以重現 2025 年的強度，故預期中短線上金價將可能持續維持在區間內震盪，惟若
後續美伊談判推展順利，隨荷莫茲海峽開放，油價回落將有利市場降息預期回升，屆時搭配來自央行
的買盤，將有望再度重啟黃金新一輪多頭走勢。

固定收益：市場觀望華許首秀，美債利率料陷整理走勢
自 2/28 美伊戰事爆發並導致荷莫茲海峽封鎖以來 ， 全球金融市場經歷自 1970 年代以來最嚴重
的供給端通膨衝擊。西德州原油與布蘭特原油價格的大幅飆升，引發市場對於通膨，乃至於 Fed 利
率政策方向轉變的疑慮，成為推升美債殖利率的核心驅動引擎。
這場由地緣政治引發的能源危機，並非僅侷限於美國單一國家，而是全球公債市場的普遍現象。
由於全球能源報價以美元計價 ， 且原油實體供給短缺屬於全球性危機 ， 主要已開發國家的公債殖利率
呈現極高相關性的同步上揚 ， 其中又以日本與英國的公債殖利率升幅最大 。 日本 75% 能源依賴中東

供給，不論在物價上或是財政體質上面臨不小壓力。
我們預期 6 月市場的總經焦點將在華許首次主持的 6/17 FOMC 利率會議。4 月下旬在分析華許
在參議院聽證會的發言時 ， 便已提出其主張可能導致 「美債殖利率陡峭化」 與 「債市波動性恐將加大」
的想法。彭博 4/30~5/13 調查顯示，118 名受訪者中有 64% 預期華許上任後，美債利率將出現某
種形式的陡峭化，其中熊市陡峭化（長率升速>短率升速）是主流觀點(佔比近 30%)。未來若債券殖
利率曲線呈現陡峭化(不論是熊市、牛市或是扭轉陡峭化)，短中天期的債券殖利率表現都將相對優於
長天期債券，加上短天期債券的存續期間本就低於長天期公債，因此當債市震盪時，短天期債券價格
表現將優於長天期債券。
巴克萊近期研究也顯示，當聯邦基金利率在 2-5% 的區間內波動，平均約為 3.6% 時，30 年
期公債殖利率與聯邦基金利率之間的利差為 170 個基點。假設長期中性利率在 3-4%的區間內（如
聯準會點陣圖所示），30 年期公債殖利率應在 4.7-5.7%區間。而目前約 5%水準約在區間相對偏低

的水準。巴克萊認為，在美國經濟韌性的背景下，目前30 年期公債殖利率還不算高，考慮到當前的
財政狀況和異常的不確定性，投資人可能需要更高的期限溢酬。若此說法成真，相較於短期債券，長
天期債券殖利率上升幅度更高(價格下跌幅度更大)。
雖然 5 月至今股市有所表現，但在中東地緣政治談判反覆，能源通膨預期以及聯準會偏鷹派立場
的交互牽引下，債券市場表現卻相對震盪，30 年期美債殖利率甚至一度觸及 2007 年以來的高位。
近來市場定價邏輯高度聚焦於「通膨風險與聯準會政策路徑」 ，債券殖利率 6 月仍將持續面臨高檔震
盪的壓力。目前債券殖利率水準明顯高於其歷史均值，我們認為站在息收 (Carry)的角度，債券仍具
長線投資價值。此外，由於預期美債殖利率曲線趨向陡峭化，再加上長天期公債本就有較高的存續期
間，故就債券價格來看，短天期公債/投資級債券的表現料將優於長天期公債/投資級債券，因此我們
仍維持債券投資聚焦中短天期公債/投資級債的建議。

---

研究員/鄭高輯 OWENCHENG@uni-psg.com

結論: 
力成法說會大致符合預期，其中: (1) 3Q26 毛利率略優於預期，稅後
EPS 符合預期 。(2) 4Q26 營收穩健成長 ，UTR 可望持續提升 。(3) FOPLP
缺機台情況有明顯改善，擴產時程不受影響。(4) 2027 年營運將由
FOPLP、FCBGA 等先進封裝驅動成長。由於營運展望與上季變化不大，
大致維持 2026、2027 年獲利預估，預估稅後 EPS 分別為 12.89 元、
18.57 元，考量力成策略佈局以先進封裝廠為定位，可望擺脫記憶體
景氣循環既定印象，故維持PER 20X，目標價 370 元，維持買進建議。

重點摘要: 
1.3Q26 毛利率略優於預期:  
力成 3Q26 營收+9.6%QoQ(+26.9%YoY)， 主要係AI 伺服器帶旺 eSSD 需
求成長 ， 且先進邏輯封裝 、 子公司超豐， 以及Tera Probe/Tera Power
均有雙位數QoQ 成長表現 。 在毛利率方面 ， 較前季提升2.2pt 至24.0%，
略優於預期的 23.5%，反映產品組合優化，且因應原物料成本提升進
行個位數~雙位數%不等的漲價。OPM 亦較前季提升 2.4pt 至 17.7%，
單季稅後 EPS 3.55 元，大致符合預期的 3.60 元。

2.4Q26 穩健成長，維持 2026 年獲利預估不變:  
展望 4Q26，預估營收持續穩定成長，預期 Dram 產能 UTR 維持高檔且
有價格效益，企業級 SSD 需求也將維持高檔，另 AI 相關應用將持續
扮演主要成長引擎，高階 FCBGA 已成功導入大晶片封裝並延續成長動
能，TPJ+TPW 因伺服器及 AI 強勁需求也將保持穩健，相較之下，超豐
在 AI、HPC 及 IoT 需求成長，但消費性電子 、PC 及 NB 拉貨轉趨保守。
目前整體封裝和測試 UTR 都超過 90%，4Q26 將隨營收增加而持續提
升。預估 2026 全年營收+27.9%YoY，稅後 EPS 12.89 元(原估 12.87
元)。

3. FOPLP 缺機台情況有明顯改善，擴產時程不受影響:  
在 FOPLP 進度方面，客戶端產品驗證持續推進，朝向 2027 量產既定
目標穩定推進，且隨著市場需求及量產信心度提升，客戶NPI 的需求
亦顯著增加。在設備交貨狀況方面，儘管半導體廠商對設備需求十分
吃緊，但 在公司與 供應商共同努力下，缺機台情況有明顯改善，
2027~2028 年擴產將按計畫進行，出貨時程不受影響。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

預計 AMD 的 AI 晶片將率先導入 FOPLP， 並於2027 年中以前如期量產，
預估第一階段產能 2K/月。

4.2027 年將由 FOPLP、FCBGA 等先進封裝驅動成長:  
展望 2027 年，力成營運成長將從過去高度仰賴記憶體產品，重塑為
以先進封裝包括 FOPLP、2.5D、3D、矽光 CPO 及高階 FCBGA 所驅動的
成長模式 。 目前先進封裝佔比30%， 預計2027 年在 FOPLP 設備問題改
善，且如期導入 AMD AI 產品下，先進封裝比重將持續提升。此外，
記憶體供需在 2027 年緩和但仍供不應求，在記憶體大廠產能陸續開
出下，有助於記憶體營收穩增並維持高稼動率。預估 2027 年營收
+22.1%YoY， 稅後淨利+45.8%YoY， 稅後EPS 18.57 元(原估 18.25 元)。

## Document 229: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

작성자(한승한)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 293: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 313: GPT-6 Astra来了，稚晖君为什么说本体越来越重要_

Source PDF: all report pdf/GPT-6 Astra来了，稚晖君为什么说本体越来越重要_.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-30

### Page 1

通⽤⼤模型开始伸⼿控制机器⼈，彭志辉的判断是可规模化的优质本体会越来越重要。智
元押注全栈，同时把⾏业的评价标准从下线台数推向上岗⼯时。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 29 ⽇，智元在上海世博中⼼办了第四届创新⽇。主论坛和会后群访⾥， GPT-6 Astra 被反复
提起。
这款通⽤⼤模型最近被证明可以直接控制机器⼈完成⼀些操作任务。市场的疑问很直接，如果
基础模型⾜够强，具⾝公司⾃研的⼤脑还有没有价值，本体会不会沦为代⼯硬件。
智元联合创始⼈、总裁兼 CTO 彭志辉（稚晖君）的回答是，恰恰相反。
他的理由是，不管⼤模型最后能不能通吃具⾝，智能都要落到⼀具可靠的⾝体上才能⼲活。能
低成本、⾼⼀致性、⼤规模造出这具⾝体的公司，并不多。
智元是⽬前跑得最快的⼀家。 2025 年智元出货 5000 多台，按 Omdia 数据占全球 40% ； 2026 年上
半年出货 9700 台， Counterpoint 统计份额超过 43% 。 9 ⽉，第 20000 台机器⼈下线，上半年收⼊
接近 20 亿元。
但彭志辉在群访⾥⾃⼰给这组数字降了温，「万台下线只是制造业的胜利，万台能够上岗才是物

理 AI 的胜利。」
01 ⼤模型越强，本体越重要
本体为什么更重要，彭志辉的推演分两种情况。
⼀种是通⽤基模真的覆盖了具⾝能⼒。⼤家拿到同样的智能，⽐拼的就是谁能以更低成本、更
⾼可靠性把它装进真实世界，规模化本体⼚商占优。
另⼀种是基模做不到真正泛化，各个垂域还要训练⾃⼰的模型。训练数据从部署中来，部署靠
本体，本体⼚商依然占优。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

两条路通向同⼀个结论，可规模化的优质本体会越来越重要。
智元合伙⼈姚卯⻘在主论坛引⽤英伟达的报告， Astra 在 120 项仿真任务⾥平均成功率 66.3% ，能
⼒集中在语义理解和动作规划。但它单次推理要 18 秒以上，涉及⼒触反馈的⾼动态精细动作，
智元测下来成功率基本为零。
彭志辉补充了另⼀个信息，近万亿参数的 Astra 跑在云端，完成⼀个简单任务可能要⼗⼏分钟甚
⾄半⼩时，机器⼈的操作却是实时的。
姚卯⻘的判断是，⼤模型会更多承担 System2 的⻓程规划， VLA 训练出的技能是被它调⽤的⼯
具，再往下是 100 到 1000Hz 的实时电机控制，这⼏层都和本体深度耦合。
本体的作⽤还体现在后训练上。智元在实验室⾥把 100 台真机组⽹，和背后的训练集群实时同
步，机器⼈边⼲活边回传数据，模型权重再下发到每⼀台。同样的训练时⻓， 4 台机器⼈和 100
台机器⼈对⽐，任务成功率从 50% 左右提到 98% 。姚卯⻘的说法是，后训练也有 Scaling ，规模
指的就是真机台数。

这条路，纯模型公司很难复制。
智元两个⽅向都在做。 GO-3 Preview 是⽬前⾏业发布的参数量最⼤的具⾝模型，在 RoboDojo 等
三个评测榜单登顶；同时智元也拿 Astra 当底座，做出了运动智能的⾃进化智能体。
在彭志辉看来，模型是必选题。不做模型，最后就会像传统⾃动驾驶⽅案公司⼀样，被⾃研模
型的主机⼚取代。
这套逻辑有⼀个前提。 Astra 的慢，来⾃参数规模和云端推理，彭志辉⾃⼰也列了解法，分层设
计、模型蒸馏、缓存系统。这些问题如果先被⼤模型公司解决，本体⼚商的窗⼝期会⽐预想的
短。
02 从参数表到⼯时表
客⼾的问题已经变了。
过去看机器⼈，看参数，看跑跳打拳的视频。现在客⼾问的是，它能稳定⼲多少活，⼲⼀次要
花多少钱。
智元给的答案是 Worken ， Work 和 Token 的组合，中⽂名就叫智元，度量机器⼈的有效智能⼯
时。计算拆成三个乘数，在岗活跃台数、⾃主⼯作时间、产出能效⽐。⼀台机器⼈每⼯作 10 分
钟就要⼈接管⼀次，⾃主⼯时就很有限。

### Page 3

收费⽅式上，彭志辉没有给单⼀答案。卖本体、卖服务、和客⼾共创运营都可以，看客⼾要的
是设备还是结果。
真正卡规模的是运维。
彭志辉的原话是，「每卖⼀台机器还要给机器⼈配⼀个保姆，这是永远不会到那⼀天的。」客⼾
愿意⼤规模买单，要等他的问题从「能不能造得出来」变成「能不能养得起」。
⻰旗是⽬前最完整的样本。这条 3C 产线 2026 年 3 ⽉规模化投产，已稳定运⾏ 7 个多⽉， 6 天直播
期间作业成功率 99.99% ，平均节拍 22 秒⼀件，和⼈⼯持平。姚卯⻘透露，智元研发团队早已撤
出现场，基础运维移交给⻰旗⾃⼰的团队， 3C 领域另有 3 家以上企业在合作。
服务场景⾛得更快。横琴⻓隆乐园部署了近 500 台机器⼈，⽇均接待游客超过 1 万⼈，开园后每
周⼀个⼩版本、每⽉⼀个⼤版本地迭代。
⼈机⽐是另⼀条线。最早⼀台机器⼈配⼀个⼯程师，后来 3 ⽐ 1 ，现在 10 ⽐ 1 ，⽬标是 20 到 30 ⽐
1 。⼈机⽐降不下来，每个场景都要堆专家，⽣意就只能按项⽬做。
姚卯⻘给了⾏业层⾯的两个刻度。⼀是部署⼀个新场景要投⼊多少⼈天，他拿⾃动驾驶类⽐，

做得好的团队 10 个⼈ 3 个⽉搞定⼀个⻋型，做得差的要 100 个⼈⼲⼀年。⼆是中国⼯⼚⾥什么时
候有 1 万台以上机器⼈⻓期稳定作业。
Worken ⽬前还是智元⾃⼰定义的⼝径，能不能变成⾏业度量衡，取决于客⼾和同⾏是否愿意按
它结算。
03 壁垒在哪⾥
彭志辉对价格战的判断很明确，本体⾃研和垂直整合能⼒不够，最后只能卷价格、卷成本。
智元的硬件路线⾛了三步。新⼀代 AHA3 架构第⼀次把运动、作业、交互三种智能放进⼀颗
SoC ，控制系统体积降 50% ，通信延迟降 90% 。核⼼零部件⾃研率超过 90% ，但最关键的芯⽚
还不是⾃研，下⼀代 AHA4 会提⾼这部分⽐例。
钱和⼈在往智能倾斜。研发团队 30% 做本体， 70% 做智能。今年训练算⼒接近万卡集群，彭志
辉说，这个规模已经在和通⽤⼤模型公司抢算⼒。

《醫療器械喜憂參半，但必要性手術需求仍佳》
近月醫療服務設備股為健護中股價表現較差 ，主因：
1)亞培受到產品責任險達成部分和解協議 ，後續還
有其他聯邦 、州判決仍為股價壓力； 2)波士頓科學
受資安事件影響生產宣告無法達成財測 ，HRS的左

心耳封堵術 (LAAC) 指南適用 WATCHMAN、 亞培
Amulet，可同時做消融+LAAC，但LAAC不能完全
替代口服抗凝血藥物 (OAC)對預防中風的效用 ，仍
為二線，等同喜憂參半 。另受自付成本增加 ，預期
擇期、外科手術需求疲軟延伸至年底 ，機器人手術
成長率區間接近 14.5%，下半年低於上半年 ，將以
擴大翻新系統的布署降低門檻及增加門診手術需求 ，
擴大目標市場 ，但必要醫療手術需求健康 ，心血管
類手術下半年仍成長，醫療器材基本面喜憂參半。
《星級獎金與醫療成本控管力使長期獲利分化》
每月聯邦醫療保險優勢計畫投保人數成長趨勢持續 ，
CMS預計於2026年10月中公布 2027年醫療優勢計
畫(MA)星級評等，因評級變嚴將影響醫保公司會員
留存率、競爭力及2028年品質獎金收入不確定性增
加，低於4星級業者面臨品質獎金減少與長期獲利承
壓風險。另無意外法案獨立爭議解決 (IDR)仲裁成本
持續推升商業保險理賠支出 ，增加2027年雇主團體
及ACA市場保費定價壓力 。管理式醫療業者今年展

現利潤優先於市占率、砍不賺錢計畫/人數拉抬利潤
率，但明年若保費調漲幅度不及醫療成本增速 ，恐
推升醫療損失率 、壓縮承保利潤 。後續獲利關鍵在
於星級評等 、保費調整能力及理賠成本控管 ，業者
間的長期獲利表現可能進一步分化。

智原 3035 TT  ( 電子–半導體 ) 
緩步前進，待評價面更具吸引力 
投資與評價 
1. 2026 年雖子公司 MCU 業務挹注，但受產能限制及認列時程影響，預估
營收將年減 26.2%。 
2. 遞延專案將於明年貢獻外 ，預估有三至四個先進製程專案進入 T ape-out
階段，但仍將面臨供應鏈成本壓力，預估 2027 營收年增 25.2%。 
3. 預估智原 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 3.27/5.39 元，考量先進封裝/製程為主
要增長動能，營收佔比高之成熟製程維持穩健下，以歷史本益比區間中
間偏上 35 倍為評價基礎，目標價 188 元(35X FY27E EPS)，股價未具顯
著吸引力，故由買進轉為中立投資建議。 
重點更新 
受限封裝產能限制及專案認列時程影響，預估 2026 年營收將年減 26.2% 
子公司 MCU 業務優於研究部先前預期，全年成長翻倍，主要受惠成熟製程
效能提升下 ，高階 MCU 配合演算法能夠完成客戶所需特定功能。雖 MCU 表
現亮眼，但高階封裝業務則受到封測產能與材料供應限制，目前透過供應鏈
協調逐步改善，加上部分案件認列時程遞延，推故將至明年上半年認列，使
整體營收表現不如研究部預期，目前累計 Design Win 訂單金額中，美國成
為 NRE 業務最大貢獻市場，亦成功取得美國客戶的三星 2nm GAA 雲端 AI
設計專案。整體 2026 年營收研究部預估將年減 26.2%，達 132.9 億元。 
2027 年先進製程/封裝為主要動能，但評價未具顯著吸引力 
除前述 26 年部分專案時程遞延至 1H27 認列外，預估上半年有三至四個先
進製程相關專案進入 T ape-out 階段，NRE 將依照合約里程碑逐步認列。其
中 2nm GAA 專案，研究部推估 NRE 合約金額約為 5000~6000 萬美元，首
筆收入亦於 1H27 依專案進度認列，後續仍有設計、流片及量產階段的長期
營收貢獻。矽電容取得 MLCC 客戶委託開發，1H27 進入 tape-out。光通訊
方面，光模組有相關MCU 客戶，光交換機相關專案執行中，CXL 預計 2H27
量產。MCU 則將透過子公司雅特力既有產品及新產品量產，維持穩定出貨，
但 2027 年仍存在供應鏈成本壓力，目前晶圓代工廠已發出自第一季起調漲
價格的通知，記憶體及封測供給也預期持續偏緊。整體而言 2027 年營收預
估年增 25.2%，達 166.3 億元，以評價面考量，股價未具顯著吸引力。

### Page 2

投資 顧問 
個股報告 
2026/10/28

訪談內容 
先進製程及封裝仍是重點 
智原未來技術發展將採取成熟製程完整平台化、先進製程依客戶需求投入的雙軌策略，核心方向是整合  IP、ASIC 
設計、晶圓代工及先進封裝，提供客戶一站式設計服務。成熟製程方面，公司已建立 40、28、22 及 14nm 相關 
IP 平台，並利用既有 14nm 技術經驗與設計資產，推進 12nm IP 開發，以降低研發成本、縮短平台建置時間。對
於更先進的製程，則依客戶需求及投資報酬率選擇性投入，避免無效的研發支出。

先進製程方面，最具代表性的進展是與三星晶圓代工合作的 2nm GAA 雲端 AI 專案。該案整合 PCIe Gen6 等先進 
IP、客製化 3D DRAM 堆疊、Hybrid Bonding 及 TSV 等技術，並結合智原既有 2.5D 封裝與 OSAT 供應鏈經驗。
客戶原先規劃使用 4nm，後因效能及功耗需求升級至 2nm，產品涉及高精度 64-bit 浮點科學運算。

Edge AI 方面，智原將結合雅特力標準MCU 與自身客製化 SoC、NPU 設計能力，涵蓋低功耗 AIoT 及高效能邊緣
推論市場。雅特力 Edge AI 相關營收目前已占其整體營收約 25%，並參與 Physical AI 相關產業聯盟。 
其他新興布局包括與聯電關注矽電容製程應用、光通訊及光交換器接收端設計，以及CXL 控制晶片。其中，矽電容
相關專案預計 2027 年陸續量產，CXL 專案則 2027 年下半年量產，整體策略是透過跨廠商技術整合，將新取得的
Design Win 逐步轉化為長期量產營收及 IP 權利金收入。

以目前公司策略來講，14nm 以上製程，主要與聯電、三星合作，在 12nm 以下，則以 Intel 與三星為核心夥伴。
亦與 GlobalFoundries 今年正式建立合作關係。

表一、智原修正預估與前次預估比較

資料來源：國泰證期研究部預估

### Page 3

投資 顧問 
個股報告 
2026/10/28

智原 ESG 政策 
智原科技主要提供特定用途的積體電路(ASIC)設計服務及矽智財(SIP)，公司名列全球前 15 大 SIP 矽智 
財供應商、全球前 50 大 ASIC 設計服務供應商，產業類別歸類在半導體行業中。依據專門為投資人設 
計的永續揭露框架 SASB 準則，半導體行業關注重點為下列幾項：溫室氣體排放、能源管理、水和廢水 
管理、廢棄物和危險材料管理、員工健康與安全、員工多元化和包容性、產品設計與生命週期管理、材 
料採購與效率。由於智原科技本身並無工廠，研究部將篇幅著重在產品設計面，並因員工對於 IC 設計 
公司而言是相當重要的資產，故員工相關議題也會著重於下描述，其餘溫室氣體及能/資源管理等則於 
後簡述。

1. 產品設計管理： 
在原料取得與生產製造部分，智原依據歐盟廢電子電機設備中危害物質禁用指令（RoHS）及歐盟化學政策
（REACH）建立綠色生產供應鏈，推行綠色採購，並制定【綠色環保政策及作業程序】及【環境物質管理作業辦法】 ，
要求所有生產供應商必須簽署環境物質不使用承諾書, 禁用危害環境物料；並且要求供應商以及上一級供應商須對有害
物質進行原材料監控與製程監控管理，並提供第三方的檢測報告以確保符合綠色產品以及國際法規。智原科技所提供
之 A S I C 產品， 符合 Eco-design 有害化學物質評估不含鎘 Cadmium(Cd)及鹵素(氯、溴) Halogen (Cl, Br)。生態
設計(Eco-design)指令中，要求能耗產品的製造商在設計階段時，有義務減少產品的耗能和負面的環境影響。該指令
的主要目的是減少能源使用、提高能源應用效率及降低對環境的衝擊，智原 PowerSlash™ IP 能在 0.81V 至 1. 32V 廣
域電壓下運作。特有的加速模式（Turbo Mode）可在額定頻率下減少 MCU 核心約 40%的動態功耗。

2. 員工健康與安全： 
智原採 TOSHMS 台灣職業安全衛生管理系統之要求，定期檢視執行成果與缺失檢討，以預防 
及降低工安發生率。依據勞動部職業安全衛生署之職業災害失能認定，若排除上下班交通意外事故，智原於 2021 年皆
無因職業所引起之疾病、傷害、失能或死亡。針對零星同仁上下班途中發生之交通意外事故，統計資料如圖一。智原
藉由安全衛生教育訓練活動，建構安全職場文化、營造健康職場氛圍、強化安衛管理目標，保障同仁安全，定期舉辦
相關職業安全訓練，針對不同類別工作場域會遇到的情況，提供不同類型的安全衛生教育訓練，相關受訓人數以及時
數如圖二。

圖一、員工意外事故比例

資料來源：智原、國泰證期研究部整理

3. 員工多元化以及包容性： 
智原致力於多元化招聘策略，積極延攬全球各地專業人才，在全球各地建立符合未來需求的 
人才庫及研發中心。全球員工來自台灣、中國、美國、加拿大、日本、韓國、印度、越南、 
馬來西亞、新加坡、亞美尼亞等國。據台灣地區《身心障礙者權益保障法》第 38 條規定，企業進用具有就業能力之身
心障礙者人數，不得低於員工總人數 1%。其中，進用重度以上身心障礙者，每進用 1 人得以 2 人核計。為保障身心
障礙人士的工作權益，智原積極配合政府政策，依法雇用身心障礙人士共 4 名 (包含重度 2 位、中度 1 位、輕度 1
位)。加權雇用比率符合法令規定，顯示智原對於弱勢族群之照顧，提供其公平就業機會，保障其就業權益。

4. 水和廢水管理： 
智原公司內無生產製程作業，故無工業廢水、工業廢氣、毒氣、化學品。產品生產委由專業代工廠製造，能源需求多
為空調、照明及伺服器設備用電等一般民生需求。用水 100%來自台灣自來水公司，主要為一般生活用水，無製程用
水。2022 年總用水量為 18,512.611 度，較 2021 年增加 276.369 度。主要原因為 2021 年 5~7 月因 COVID-19 疫情
影響，實施分流上班，致辦公室用水量減少，2022 年疫情趨緩回歸正常上班型態，智原持續推動各項節水措施與宣
導，減少及管控水資源消耗。

圖三、智原 2020~2022 年用水量統計

資料來源:智原、國泰證期研究部整理

5. 溫室氣體排放： 
智原於 2022 年 10 月 14 日完成總部大樓屋頂建置太陽能發電系統自建自用，裝置容量 89.70KWp，產生電力供
給廠內使用，統計至 2022 年 12 月 31 日產電量 20,122 度，減碳量 10.242 噸 COe，預估年平均發電量約
93,731 度(年減碳量達 47.709 噸 COe)。未來持續規劃節能減碳措施及執行，中長期視後續減碳狀況評估綠電採
購，確保達成 2050 年溫室氣體淨零排放目標。

## Document 483: セルビアは政権交代へ

Source PDF: all report pdf/セルビアは政権交代へ.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 27 日

MURC Focus

セルビアは政権交代へ

～揺り戻しの中で問われる対中関係のリバランス～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 総選挙で「学生リスト」が勝利 
西バルカンのセルビアで10 月 25 日、総選挙（国民議会の一院制、定数250 議席、比例代表制）が行われた。
即日開票の結果、先に辞任して総選挙に臨んだアレクサンダル・ヴチッチ前大統領は、自身が率いるセルビア進
歩党（SNS）を中心とする与党連合（統一セルビア）の敗北を認めた。代わって、ヴチッチ政権の汚職体質を批判
してきた学生を中心とする政治団体「学生リスト」が、国民の不満の受け皿となって勝利した（図表1）。

この選挙は、ヴチッチ前大統領に対する、実質的な信任投票の性格を持っていた。経済面では、 他国と同様
に、近年のセルビアのインフレは深刻だった 。一方、中国や欧州連合（ EU）からの相次いだ企業進出や人口動
態に伴う構造的な人手不足、政権による分配政策（最低賃金の引き上げなど）の影響もあって、経済は物価・賃
金スパイラルの様相を呈するに至り、国民の不満は高まっていた（図表 2）。 
図表 1. 開票結果 図表 2. セルビアのインフレと雇用

（注）開票率 96.7%時点。
